To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (H and O), we need to identify all possible paths between these nodes and find the smallest set of nodes whose removal will eliminate all paths.

Step-by-step analysis:

1. **Identify the two black nodes (H and O).**
2. **Examine the immediate neighboring nodes of H and O:**
   - Node H is connected to nodes G, I, and C.
   - Node O is connected to nodes C, F, Y, J, and P.

3. **Iteratively find other neighboring nodes:**
   - For node G (neighbor of H): connected to E, L, and X.
   - For node I (neighbor of H): connected to P, C, and J.
   - For node C (neighbor of H): connected to O, Y, N, B, and I.
   - For node F (neighbor of O): connected to X, E, K, M, and J.
   - For node Y (neighbor of O): connected to N and C.
   - For node J (neighbor of O): connected to P, I, K, and F.
   - For node P (neighbor of O): connected to I, J, and M.

4. **Evaluate the found paths:**
   - Paths between H and O include:
     - H -> C -> O
     - H -> I -> P -> O
     - H -> I -> J -> O
     - H -> G -> E -> F -> O
     - H -> G -> E -> X -> K -> J -> O
     - H -> G -> E -> X -> F -> J -> O
     - H -> G -> E -> X -> F -> O

   To completely disconnect H and O, we need to remove nodes that are common in these paths or form bottlenecks.

   By analyzing the paths, we see that removing nodes C, I, and J will eliminate all possible paths between H and O.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed is 3.

Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [C, I, J]

   - Observing all the paths, node "B" appears in every single path.

     - Removing "M" or "P" would cut multiple paths from "E" to "I" or "H".
     - Removing these strategic nodes disrupts all potential routes better. 

Given these observations, the optimal cut set must consider crucial nodes along multiple paths.
